- Source: `prob-21380` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=21380) quote: "or within 90 days before or after that period"
- Also: `bernard` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2600573/bernard-v-foley/) quote: "care custodians"

**es27** (both, abuse). Lynelle Goodreau said her uncle Sheldon abused and isolated her grandmother, Laura Lowrie, and got her to change her trust. Could a granddaughter bring the elder abuse case?
- Choices: No, the trustee had to / **Yes, she had standing** / Her parents did
- Fix: If you suspect elder abuse in your family, report it and get a lawyer. A grandchild may be able to act.
- Source: `lowrie` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2251837/in-re-estate-of-lowrie/) quote: "the granddaughter has standing to bring this elder abuse civil lawsuit"

**es28** (both, abuse). Lynelle asked the court, under Probate Code 259, to treat Sheldon as if he had died before his mother. What is that rule for?
- Choices: Speeding up probate / **Abusers losing inheritance** / Cutting estate taxes
- Fix: Abuse can cost an heir the inheritance. Keep records and report it.
- Source: `prob-259` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=259) quote: "shall be deemed to have predeceased a decedent"
- Also: `lowrie` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2251837/in-re-estate-of-lowrie/) quote: "a finding pursuant to Probate Code section 259 that Sheldon was deemed to have predeceased decedent"

**es29** (both, abuse). In Lowrie, what did the petition say Sheldon did that is a classic elder abuse warning sign?
- Choices: Moved her abroad / **Cut her off from family** / Paid her bills late
- Fix: Watch for a helper who blocks visits and calls. Isolation is a red flag; report it.
- Source: `lowrie` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2251837/in-re-estate-of-lowrie/) quote: "Sheldon isolated Decedent from her two other children, her five grandchildren"

**es30** (both, poa). The day after doctors said Nancy Powell could not act for herself, her son Jeffrey used her power of attorney to give her partnership interest to a charitable trust. Was the gift valid?
- Choices: Yes, a POA allows gifts / **No, it was not allowed** / With a notary, yes
- Fix: If you want your agent to make gifts, say so in the document, with limits. Otherwise the agent cannot.
- Source: `powell` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4563430/estate-of-powell-v-commr/) quote: "did not authorize J to make gifts in excess of the annual Federal gift tax exclusion"
- Also: `prob-4264` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=4264) quote: "Make or revoke a gift of the principal's property in trust or otherwise"

**es31** (both, poa). Under California Probate Code 4264, when may an agent under a power of attorney make a gift of your property?
- Choices: When the family agrees / **If the POA says so** / A bank says so
- Fix: Read the POA before you sign it. Gift powers should be spelled out, or left out on purpose.
- Source: `prob-4264` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=4264) quote: "only if the power of attorney expressly grants that authority to the attorney-in-fact"
- Also: `powell` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4563430/estate-of-powell-v-commr/) quote: "did not have authority under the POA to make gifts"

**es32** (both, poa). Your aunt makes you her agent under a power of attorney. What duty does California law put on you?
- Choices: Share it with family / **Act only for her** / Follow the heirs' wishes
- Fix: Keep her money separate, keep receipts, and use it only for her.
- Source: `prob-4232` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=4232) quote: "has a duty to act solely in the interest of the principal and to avoid conflicts of interest"
- Also: `powell` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4563430/estate-of-powell-v-commr/) quote: "purportedly acting on behalf of decedent under a power of attorney"

**es33** (adult, business). As Albert Strangi's health failed, about $10 million of his assets went into a family limited partnership. His estate then paid tax only on his partnership interest. Did that work?
- Choices: Yes, the discount held / **No, assets were included** / The house alone counted
- Fix: A family entity needs a real business purpose and must be run like one. Get tax advice before you set one up.
- Source: `strangi` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/791260/albert-strangi-deceased-rosalie-gulig-independent-v-commissioner-of/) quote: "lacked a substantial non-tax purpose"
- Also: `usc-26-2036` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/2036) quote: "the possession or enjoyment of, or the right to the income from, the property"

**es34** (adult, business). In Strangi, which exception to section 2036 could have kept the partnership assets out of the estate?
- Choices: An LLC name / **A bona fide sale** / Filing it out of state
- Fix: Ask what real reason, besides tax, the entity serves. Without one, the IRS can pull assets back in.
- Source: `strangi` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/791260/albert-strangi-deceased-rosalie-gulig-independent-v-commissioner-of/) quote: "bona fide sale"
- Also: `usc-26-2036` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/2036) quote: "except in case of a bona fide sale for an adequate and full consideration"

**es35** (adult, business). Like Nancy Powell, a parent moves assets into a family partnership but keeps the right, with others, to dissolve it. What does federal estate tax law do?
- Choices: It leaves her estate / **It counts in her estate** / It becomes tax free
- Fix: Keeping control or the income usually keeps the assets in your estate. Plan with a tax pro.
- Source: `powell` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4563430/estate-of-powell-v-commr/) quote: "justified the application of section 2036(a)(2)"
- Also: `usc-26-2036` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/2036) quote: "the right, either alone or in conjunction with any person, to designate the persons who shall possess or enjoy the property"

**es36** (both, property). Frankie Valli used community funds to buy a $3.75 million life policy, naming his wife Randy as sole owner and beneficiary. At divorce, whose was it?
- Choices: Randy's; her name is on it / **It is community property** / Frankie alone owns it
- Fix: If you mean to change who owns something in a marriage, sign a clear written agreement.
- Source: `valli` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2674462/marr-of-valli/) quote: "unless the statutory transmutation requirements have been met, the life insurance policy is community property"
- Also: `fam-852` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=FAM&sectionNum=852) quote: "is not valid unless made in writing by an express declaration"

**es37** (both, property). After Valli, what does it take in California to turn community property into one spouse's separate property?
- Choices: Putting it in one name / **An express writing** / A spoken promise
- Fix: Write it down, state the change plainly, and have the spouse who loses out sign.
- Source: `fam-852` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=FAM&sectionNum=852) quote: "is not valid unless made in writing by an express declaration"
- Also: `valli` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2674462/marr-of-valli/) quote: "naming wife as the sole owner and beneficiary"

**es38** (both, property). When a married Californian dies, how much of the couple's community property already belongs to the surviving spouse?
- Choices: It goes to the kids / **One-half of it** / One-quarter of it
- Fix: Your will controls only your half of community property. Plan with your spouse.
- Source: `prob-100` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=100) quote: "one-half of the community property belongs to the surviving spouse and the other one-half belongs to the decedent"
- Also: `valli` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2674462/marr-of-valli/) quote: "community property"

**es39** (both, property). Frances Riddle owned a home in joint tenancy with her husband. She wanted her half to pass by her will instead. She signed a deed from herself to herself and died 20 days later. Did it work?
- Choices: No, he had to sign too / **Yes, it ended it** / Yes, but a year later
- Fix: Know how each property is titled. Joint tenancy passes to the survivor unless it is ended first.
- Source: `riddle` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2109417/riddle-v-harmon/) quote: "Mrs. Riddle died 20 days later"

**es40** (both, property). In Riddle, the trial court first gave the whole house to Frances's husband. Why does joint tenancy usually do that?
- Choices: Wills cover all property / **The survivor takes it** / Probate splits it
- Fix: Check your deed. A will does not control joint tenancy property unless the joint tenancy was ended first.
- Source: `riddle` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2109417/riddle-v-harmon/) quote: "the right of survivorship by conveyance of his or her joint tenancy interest"

**es41** (both, wills). Like the beneficiary rule in Sveen, California has a will rule. After a divorce, what happens to gifts to your ex in a will you signed while married?
- Choices: The ex still gets them / **They are all revoked** / They pass to the state
- Fix: Make a new will after a divorce anyway, so there is no doubt who you want.
- Source: `prob-6122` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=6122) quote: "Any disposition or appointment of property made by the will to the former spouse"
- Also: `sveen` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4505999/sveen-v-melin/) quote: "revokes any revocable"

**es42** (both, trusts). Barefoot involved late changes to a revocable trust. If the trust is silent on how to revoke it, what does California law allow?
- Choices: A phone call / **A signed writing** / A new will, by itself
- Fix: Follow the method in your trust, in writing. If it names one exclusive method, use that one.
- Source: `prob-15401` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=15401) quote: "By a writing, other than a will, signed by the settlor"
- Also: `barefoot` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4714805/barefoot-v-jennings/) quote: "trust provisions or amendments"

**es43** (both, probate). Prince's estate spent years in court. In California, what can heirs use for a small estate instead of full probate?
- Choices: Full probate is a must / **A small estate affidavit** / A note from the bank
- Fix: Ask about the small estate affidavit. Check today's dollar limit and wait the required 40 days.
- Source: `prob-13100` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=13100) quote: "if 40 days have elapsed since the death of the decedent"
- Also: `ap-prince` (https://apnews.com/article/entertainment-business-minnesota-personal-taxes-internal-revenue-service-e46d73374aea6bac533cb9225fa02451) quote: "The six-year legal battle over pop superstar Prince's estate has ended"

**es44** (both, wills). Aretha Franklin's wills turned up only after a niece searched the house. In California, if a will last kept by the person cannot be found after death, what is presumed?
- Choices: It never existed / **She revoked it** / A lawyer has the original
- Fix: Keep the original will somewhere safe that your executor knows about.
- Source: `prob-6124` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=6124) quote: "it is presumed that the testator destroyed the will with intent to revoke it"
- Also: `ap-aretha` (https://apnews.com/article/aretha-franklin-will-dispute-d7aba286b05ea0d0e6318ce6abc887d5) quote: "emerged in 2019 when a niece scoured the home for records"

**es45** (both, beneficiary). Mark Sveen named his two children as backup beneficiaries. Under Minnesota's divorce rule, who takes the money when the ex is removed?
- Choices: The ex, anyway / **The backup beneficiaries** / The insurance company
- Fix: Always name a backup beneficiary on every policy and account.
- Source: `sveen` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4505999/sveen-v-melin/) quote: "will instead go to the contingent beneficiary"

**es46** (both, wills). Frances Black wrote her will by hand on three pages of a store-bought printed will form, using bits of its printed words. Probate was denied over the printed parts. What did the California Supreme Court do?
- Choices: Agreed; the printed words void it / **Reversed; it was a valid will** / Sent it to a jury
- Fix: If you use a form, write every gift and your signature in your own hand, and date it.
- Source: `black` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1222943/estate-of-black/) quote: "none of the incorporated material is either material to the substance of the will or essential to its validity"
- Also: `prob-6111` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PROB&sectionNum=6111) quote: "as part of a commercially printed form will"

**es47** (both, wills). In Estate of Black, who wrote the handwriting on the form, including the date and her name?
- Choices: A lawyer, for her / **Frances Black herself** / Her neighbor, as witness
- Fix: For a handwritten will, write it yourself. Your hand on the key parts is what counts.
- Source: `black` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1222943/estate-of-black/) quote: "all of the handwriting, including the date and her name, is that of the testatrix"

**es48** (both, wills). On a charity donor card, Dorothy Southworth wrote that her whole estate should go to the charity, then signed and dated it. Was the card her will?
- Choices: Yes, it was signed and dated / **No, it was not a will** / Part went to charity
- Fix: Put your wishes in a real will or trust. A card or letter about a future gift may not count.
- Source: `southworth` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2257204/estate-of-southworth/) quote: "Does her handwriting on the donor card constitute a holographic will? No."

**es49** (both, wills). Why did the Southworth court say the donor card failed as a will?
- Choices: It had no witnesses / **It showed future intent** / It was not dated
- Fix: Use words that make the gift now, at death, in a signed will. "I plan to" is not enough.
- Source: `southworth` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2257204/estate-of-southworth/) quote: "an intent to make a future gift to the charity"

**es50** (both, trusts). After a stroke, Wayne Andersen amended his trust to leave 60 percent to his partner, Pauline Hunt. The probate court judged his capacity by the contract standard. Was that the right test?
- Choices: Yes, trusts are contracts / **No, the will standard applied** / No test is needed
- Fix: For a simple trust change that works like a will, the will-making capacity test applies. Get a doctor's note if capacity may be questioned.
- Source: `andersen` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/5810338/andersen-v-hunt/) quote: "instead of the standard of testamentary capacity set out in Probate Code section 6100.5"

**es51** (both, trusts). In Andersen v. Hunt, the appeals court upheld Wayne's trust amendments. What did it still let stand against Pauline?
- Choices: The whole trust, as is / **The account and policy rulings** / A fine for undue influence
- Fix: Opening joint accounts and changing a beneficiary can be judged by a stricter test. Make big changes while you are well.
- Source: `andersen` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/5810338/andersen-v-hunt/) quote: "lacked capacity to open joint tenancy accounts and to change the beneficiary of his life insurance policy"

**es52** (both, beneficiary). David Egelhoff had a Boeing life policy and a pension plan, both naming his ex-wife. Which did the federal rule in Egelhoff cover?
- Choices: The life policy alone / **Both of the plans** / The pension alone
- Fix: Update the form on every plan: insurance, pension, 401(k) and IRA.
- Source: `egelhoff` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/118416/egelhoff-v-egelhoff-ex-rel-breiner/) quote: "Both plans were governed by ERISA"

**es53** (both, beneficiary). A state law let Warren Hillman's widow sue his ex-wife to hand over the insurance money. What did the Supreme Court say about that remedy?
- Choices: It worked as a backup / **It was pre-empted** / Part could be taken
- Fix: Do not count on a lawsuit to fix an old form. Update it while you can.
- Source: `hillman` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/873660/hillman-v-maretta/) quote: "We hold that it is."

**es54** (both, beneficiary). After his divorce, William Kennedy did file a new form naming his daughter Kari on one DuPont plan. Which one?
- Choices: The savings plan (SIP) / **His pension plan** / His life policy
- Fix: Make a list of every account with a beneficiary and update each one separately.
- Source: `kennedy` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/145914/kennedy-v-plan-administrator-for-dupont-savings-investment-plan/) quote: "as the beneficiary under DuPont's Pension and Retirement Plan"

## Legal Traps (`legal`)

**lg01** (both, driving). Steven Spriggs, stuck in Fresno County traffic, held his phone to check a map. The court threw out his ticket. Could he do that today?
- Choices: Yes, the Spriggs ruling still holds / **No, the law changed after** / Yes, but in heavy traffic
- Fix: Mount the phone before you drive. Spriggs read the old law, which only banned talking while holding the phone.
- Source: `spriggs` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2655025/people-v-spriggs/) quote: "At the trial held before a Fresno County Superior Court traffic commissioner, both Spriggs and the California Highway Patrol officer who issued the citation testified that Spriggs was cited for looking at a map on his cellular telephone while holding the telephone in his hand and driving."
- Also: `veh-23123-5` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=23123.5) quote: "(e) This section does not apply to an emergency services professional using an electronic wireless communications device while operating an authorized emergency vehicle, as defined in Section 165, in the course and scope of his or her duties."

**lg02** (both, driving). Nathaniel Porter held his phone in his left hand and looked at a map while driving in Santa Clara County. Was that a violation?
- Choices: Not unless he typed something / **Yes, viewing it counts** / When he was over the limit
- Fix: Use a mount and voice directions. Under Porter, looking at an app on a phone in your hand is "operating" it.
- Source: `porter` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/10598135/people-v-porter/) quote: "A trial was held before a Santa Clara County Superior Court traffic commissioner, where both Porter and the police officer agreed Porter was looking at a mapping application on his cellular telephone while holding the phone in his left hand and driving."
- Also: `veh-23123-5` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=23123.5) quote: "(2) The driver's hand is used to activate or deactivate a feature or function of the handheld wireless telephone or wireless communications device with the motion of a single swipe or tap of the driver's finger."

**lg03** (both, driving). After Porter lost his appeal over a handheld map, a friend asks how to use a phone map legally while driving in California. What works?
- Choices: Hold it low, below the window / **Mount it, one tap or swipe** / Use it on side streets only
- Fix: Mount it on the dash or windshield corner and limit it to a single tap or swipe.
- Source: `veh-23123-5` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=23123.5) quote: "(2) The driver's hand is used to activate or deactivate a feature or function of the handheld wireless telephone or wireless communications device with the motion of a single swipe or tap of the driver's finger."
- Also: `porter` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/10598135/people-v-porter/) quote: "Section 23123.5(c) also allows a driver to interact with their mounted phone with \"a single swipe or tap,\" but section 23123.5(a) does not include language regarding a single swipe or tap."

**lg04** (both, driving). Esteban Jimenez fled police, ran onto a sidewalk and hit a cinder block wall, then ran. No one else was hurt. Was that still hit-and-run?
- Choices: No, a person must be hurt / **Yes, property damage counts** / When a car was hit, yes
- Fix: After hitting anything, stop. Find the owner or leave a note with your name and address, and call police.
- Source: `jimenez-20002` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/9472686/people-v-jimenez/) quote: "Eventually, the Corolla came to a stop when it drove onto the sidewalk and collided with a short cinder block wall, causing a large cloud of smoke and debris."
- Also: `veh-20002` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=20002) quote: "(a) The driver of any vehicle involved in an accident resulting only in damage to any property, including vehicles, shall immediately stop the vehicle at the nearest location that will not impede traffic or otherwise jeopardize the safety of other motorists."

**lg05** (both, driving). Barry Halajian said his truck was only for private use, so license and registration rules did not apply. Deputies impounded it. Who won?
- Choices: Halajian, private trucks are exempt / **The tow company, it was lawful** / No one, the case was dropped
- Fix: Keep a valid license and registration. "Private travel" arguments lose, and your car can be towed and held until fees are paid.
- Source: `halajian` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/5811308/halajian-v-d-b-towing/) quote: "In the published portions of this opinion, we conclude that the sheriff's department's impounding of the truck did not violate plaintiff's right to travel, was not an unreasonable seizure, and did not misapply the Vehicle Code to his noncommercial use of the truck."
- Also: `veh-12500` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=VEH&sectionNum=12500) quote: "(a) A person may not drive a motor vehicle upon a highway, unless the person then holds a valid driver's license issued under this code, except those persons who are expressly exempted under this code."

**lg06** (both, fault). Nga Li turned left across traffic and was hit by a speeding Yellow Cab. She was partly at fault too. What did the California Supreme Court decide?
- Choices: Her own fault bars recovery / **Damages split by share of fault** / The cab pays the full bill
- Fix: Even if you made a mistake, you can still recover. Your award is cut by your share of the fault.
- Source: `li-yellowcab` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1139343/nga-li-v-yellow-cab-co/) quote: "As we have indicated, this reexamination leads us to the conclusion that the \"all-or-nothing\" rule of contributory negligence can be and ought to be superseded by a rule which assesses liability in proportion to fault."

**lg07** (both, fault). After a crash, the other driver's insurer says, "You were 20% at fault, so under California law you get zero." What does Li v. Yellow Cab say?
- Choices: That is right, zero / **You can recover 80%** / Half, by default
- Fix: Push back. California uses pure comparative fault, so a driver 20% at fault can still recover the other 80%.
- Source: `li-yellowcab` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1139343/nga-li-v-yellow-cab-co/) quote: "The first of these, the so-called \"pure\" form of comparative negligence, apportions liability in direct proportion to fault in all cases."

**lg08** (both, fault). Lisa Torti pulled her friend Alexandra Van Horn from a crashed car. Van Horn said the move left her paralyzed and sued. What did the court rule in 2008?
- Choices: Rescuers are immune / **Shield was for medical care only** / She had committed a crime
- Fix: Call 911 first. Move a person only if there is real danger, like fire or traffic.
- Source: `vanhorn` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2627382/van-horn-v-watson/) quote: "Torti does not contend that she rendered emergency medical care and she may not, therefore, claim the immunity in section 1799.102."

**lg09** (both, fault). After the Van Horn ruling against rescuer Lisa Torti, what did the Legislature change in the Good Samaritan law?
- Choices: Lawmakers left it just the same / **It now covers nonmedical help** / Rescuing became mandatory
- Fix: Today, unpaid good faith help at the scene, medical or nonmedical, is shielded from civil damages, except for gross negligence or willful or wanton misconduct.
- Source: `hsc-1799-102` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=HSC&sectionNum=1799.102) quote: "other than an act or omission constituting gross negligence or willful or wanton misconduct"
- Also: `vanhorn` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2627382/van-horn-v-watson/) quote: "Disposition In light of the foregoing reasons, we conclude that the Legislature intended for section 1799.102 to immunize from liability for civil damages only those persons who in good faith render emergency medical care at the scene of a medical emergency."

**lg10** (both, fault). Bradley Johnson was on a ladder fixing a swamp cooler when the owners' puppy jumped and its jaws closed on his trousers. No wound. Does the dog bite law apply?
- Choices: No, there must be a wound / **Yes, a bite is a bite** / For a known vicious dog
- Fix: Report it and get checked. A bite without a wound can still fall under the strict liability statute.
- Source: `johnson-mcmahan` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2302331/johnson-v-mcmahan/) quote: "The sole issue in this case is whether the California dog bite statute, Civil Code section 3342, applies when the bitee does not suffer a wound."
- Also: `civ-3342` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=3342) quote: "(a) The owner of any dog is liable for the damages suffered by any person who is bitten by the dog while in a public place or lawfully in a private place, including the property of the owner of the dog, regardless of the former viciousness of the dog or the owner's knowledge of such viciousness."

**lg11** (both, fault). Marta Priebe, a kennel worker, was badly bitten by a boarded dog. She sued its owner under the strict dog bite law. Did it apply?
- Choices: Yes, the owner is liable / **Not for a kennel worker** / When the dog bit before, yes
- Fix: If you work with dogs for pay, know the risk is mostly yours. Ask about the dog's history and use safe handling.
- Source: `priebe` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2599903/priebe-v-nelson/) quote: "The Court of Appeal concluded the doctrine should apply, finding Priebe assumed the risk of being bitten by dogs boarded at the kennel by virtue of the nature of her occupation as a kennel worker."

**lg12** (both, privacy). Brokers at an Atlanta office taped calls with California clients without telling them. Georgia allows it. Whose law applied?
- Choices: Georgia law, where the firm sat / **California law, for its residents** / Federal law across states
- Fix: If you are in California, secret taping by an out-of-state caller can still break California law. Ask "Is this recorded?"
- Source: `kearney` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2633480/kearney-v-salomon-smith-barney-inc/) quote: "Apparently in the course of the litigation of those claims, Kearney and Levy learned that numerous telephone calls that were made and received by SSB's Atlanta office to and from California clients, while the clients were in California, were tape-recorded by SSB employees without the clients' knowledge or consent."

**lg13** (both, privacy). In Flanagan, a family fight was over secretly taped phone calls. When is a talk "confidential" under California's recording law?
- Choices: When someone says it is secret / **When one expects no one overhears** / Never on a phone line
- Fix: Assume a private call is confidential. Get consent from everyone before you record it.
- Source: `flanagan` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2600714/flanagan-v-flanagan/) quote: "One line of authority holds that a conversation is confidential if a party to that conversation has an objectively reasonable expectation that the conversation is not being overheard or recorded."
- Also: `pen-632` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PEN&sectionNum=632) quote: "(d) Except as proof in an action or prosecution for violation of this section, evidence obtained as a result of eavesdropping upon or recording a confidential communication in violation of this section is not admissible in any judicial, administrative, legislative, or other proceeding."

**lg14** (both, privacy). Per Flanagan v. Flanagan, what can a person secretly taped on a confidential call ask for in civil court?
- Choices: Criminal penalties alone / **$5,000 or 3x damages** / A formal apology letter
- Fix: Save proof of the recording and talk to a lawyer. Civil penalties are available on top of the crime.
- Source: `flanagan` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2600714/flanagan-v-flanagan/) quote: "(a).) 1 Violation of the law is a misdemeanor (ibid.) and may entail a civil penalty of $5,000 or three times the actual damages, whichever is greater (§ 637.2)."
- Also: `pen-637-2` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PEN&sectionNum=637.2) quote: "Five thousand dollars ($5,000) per violation"

**lg15** (both, privacy). Kamila Malinowski had dash cam videos of her ex with their kids at custody exchanges. The trial court threw them out as illegal recordings. Was that right?
- Choices: Yes, they were secret tapes / **No, they were not confidential** / Audio alone was banned
- Fix: Talks in a car at a public exchange may not be "confidential." Ask a family law lawyer before you rely on a video.
- Source: `malinowski` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/10050351/malinowski-v-martin/) quote: "(2) no \"confidential communications\" were captured given the public nature of the exchange location;"

**lg16** (both, privacy). In 2010 Jason and Jolynn May drove around grabbing packages off porches and from community mailboxes. What crime did they plead to?
- Choices: Municipal infraction fine / **Federal mail theft** / No crime, porches are public
- Fix: Never take a package that is not yours. If one lands at your door by mistake, return it or hand it to the carrier.
- Source: `us-may` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/820330/united-states-v-jolynn-may/) quote: "they looked for packages on porches, in doorways, or in community mailboxes"
- Also: `usc-18-1708` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/18/1708) quote: "Code § 1708 - Theft or receipt of stolen mail matter gen­erally U.S."

**lg17** (both, renting). Lisa Granberry's class of tenants sued a landlord over late deposit returns. How long does a California landlord have to return a deposit or explain what was kept?
- Choices: Within sixty days / **Within three weeks** / No set deadline exists
- Fix: Send a written request. The refund or an itemized list is due within 21 days of move-out.
- Source: `granberry` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1436586/granberry-v-islay-investments/) quote: "An important provision of our statutory landlord-tenant law provides that within three weeks after the termination of tenancy a landlord must return the security deposit paid by a former tenant and provide a written accounting of any portion retained as compensation for unpaid rent, repairs, and cleaning."
- Also: `civ-1950-5` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1950.5) quote: "No later than 21 calendar days after the tenant has vacated the premises"

**lg18** (both, renting). In Granberry, the landlord missed the deposit deadline in good faith. Could it still sue the tenants for real unpaid rent and repairs?
- Choices: No, it forfeits its claims / **Yes, it can still sue** / For half the amount
- Fix: Do not count on a missed deadline. Pay what you truly owe, and keep move-out photos and receipts.
- Source: `granberry` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1436586/granberry-v-islay-investments/) quote: "We conclude that a good faith failure to comply with section 1950.5, subdivision (f), does not bar a landlord from recovering damages for unpaid *742rent, repairs, and cleaning, and we agree with the Court of Appeal to the extent that it so held."

**lg19** (both, renting). Marina Lukovsky rented a Santa Monica condo since 1994. Her landlord wanted weekend open houses to sell it. Did the court allow them?
- Choices: Never on weekends / **Yes, 1 to 4:30 weekends** / Weekday nights only
- Fix: You can ask for limits, but showings to buyers are allowed in normal business hours with proper notice.
- Source: `dromy` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1039474/dromy-v-lukovsky/) quote: "Dromy argues that the statute does not preclude weekend open houses because it states nothing about the days on which a landlord may exhibit a dwelling, but merely limits the hours he or she may do so."
- Also: `civ-1954` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1954) quote: "(d) (1) Except as provided in subdivision (e), or as provided in paragraph (2) or (3), the landlord shall give the tenant reasonable notice in writing of his or her intent to enter and enter only during normal business hours."

**lg20** (both, renting). In Dromy, the landlord won the right to show the condo. What must a landlord still give a tenant before entering for a showing?
- Choices: No notice, he owns the place / **Written or 24-hour oral notice** / A key for the new owner
- Fix: Expect written notice with date, time and purpose. For a showing to buyers, a call 24 hours ahead is enough if you got a written for-sale notice in the last 120 days.
- Source: `civ-1954` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1954) quote: "the notice may be given orally, in person or by telephone, if the landlord or his or her agent has notified the tenant in writing within 120 days"
- Also: `dromy` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1039474/dromy-v-lukovsky/) quote: "Thus when a landlord seeks to exhibit a leased dwelling unit to prospective or actual purchasers, he or she may only enter the property during reasonable hours, unless the tenant consents to another time."

**lg21** (both, work). Colin Cochran had to use his own cell phone for work calls. His plan had unlimited minutes. Did the company owe him anything?
- Choices: No, unlimited plans are free / **A fair share of the bill** / Overseas calls only
- Fix: Ask in writing for a reasonable share of your phone bill if your job needs your phone.
- Source: `cochran` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2717454/cochran-v-schwans-home-service-inc/) quote: "Whether the employees have cell phone plans with unlimited minutes or limited minutes, the reimbursement owed is a reasonable percentage of their cell phone bills."
- Also: `lab-2802` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=2802) quote: "(c) For purposes of this section, the term \"necessary expenditures or losses\" shall include all reasonable costs, including, but not limited to, attorney's fees incurred by the employee enforcing the rights granted by this section."

**lg22** (both, work). Frank Gattuso and other sales reps drove their own cars and got no mileage pay, only commissions. Can a higher commission count as repayment?
- Choices: Never, mileage checks only / **Yes, if the extra can be traced** / Yes, commissions count
- Fix: Ask your employer to show which part of your pay is for car costs. If they cannot, ask to be repaid.
- Source: `gattuso` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2625135/gattuso-v-harte-hanks-shoppers-inc/) quote: "provided there is a means or method to apportion the enhanced compensation to determine what amount is being paid for labor performed and what amount is reimbursement for business expenses"

**lg23** (both, work). At a 1999 meeting, Sharron Grant-Burton joined other directors comparing how their bonuses worked. She was fired, partly for that. Was that allowed?
- Choices: Yes, pay talk is private / **No, that firing broke policy** / In a union shop only
- Fix: You may discuss your pay. If you are punished for it, write down what happened and report it.
- Source: `grant-burton` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2285001/grant-burton-v-covenant-care-inc/) quote: "Westbrook said that the discharge was based on what Grant-Burton had said at the February 4 meeting."
- Also: `lab-232` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=232) quote: "(b) Require an employee to sign a waiver or other document that purports to deny the employee the right to disclose the amount of his or her wages."

**lg24** (both, work). Amanza Smith worked one day as a hair model at a L'Oreal show for $500. When was her pay due?
- Choices: On the next regular payday / **Right when the job ended** / Within 30 days after
- Fix: For a one-day or fixed job, ask to be paid at the end. Late pay can bring waiting time penalties.
- Source: `smith-loreal` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2633518/smith-v-superior-court/) quote: "Section 201 of the Labor Code 1 provides that if an employer \"discharges\" an employee, wages earned and unpaid at the time of discharge are due and payable immediately."
- Also: `lab-201` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=201) quote: "(a) If an employer discharges an employee, the wages earned and unpaid at the time of discharge are due and payable immediately."

**lg25** (both, work). In the L'Oreal hair model case, what can a worker seek when an employer willfully pays final wages late?
- Choices: The wages and no more / **Up to 30 days' extra pay** / A full year of extra pay
- Fix: File with the Labor Commissioner. Penalty pay runs a day's wage per day, capped at 30 days.
- Source: `lab-203` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=LAB&sectionNum=203) quote: "but the wages shall not continue for more than 30 days."
- Also: `smith-loreal` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2633518/smith-v-superior-court/) quote: "Under section 203, an employer's willful failure to pay wages to a \"discharged\" employee in accordance with section 201 subjects the employer to penalties."

**lg26** (both, work). Raymond Edwards signed a deal at Arthur Andersen barring him from serving certain clients after he left. Was it valid in California?
- Choices: Yes, because it was narrow / **No, it was never valid** / Yes, for one year
- Fix: Protect real trade secrets, but a noncompete itself will not hold in California.
- Source: `edwards` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/5608069/edwards-v-arthur-andersen-llp/) quote: "Andersen would not release Edwards, or any other employee, from the noncompetition agreement unless that employee signed the TONC."
- Also: `bpc-16600` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=BPC&sectionNum=16600) quote: "Arthur Andersen LLP (2008) 44 Cal.4th 937, to void the application of any noncompete agreement in an employment context, or any noncompete clause in an employment contract, no matter how narrowly tailored, that does not satisfy an exception in this chapter."

**lg27** (both, work). After Edwards v. Arthur Andersen, an employer says its noncompete is "narrowly tailored" so it works. What does California law now say?
- Choices: Narrow ones are still valid / **Void, however narrow** / Valid if paired with a raise
- Fix: Take the new job. Return company files and keep secrets, but the noncompete does not bind you.
- Source: `bpc-16600` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=BPC&sectionNum=16600) quote: "Arthur Andersen LLP (2008) 44 Cal.4th 937, to void the application of any noncompete agreement in an employment context, or any noncompete clause in an employment contract, no matter how narrowly tailored, that does not satisfy an exception in this chapter."
- Also: `edwards` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/5608069/edwards-v-arthur-andersen-llp/) quote: "the noncompetition agreement did not violate section 16600 because it was narrowly tailored"

**lg28** (both, consumer). Raul Figueroa's new Dodge Ram kept overheating. He took it back six to eight times, and the maker refused to buy it back. What did the jury find?
- Choices: Keep fixing it, that is it / **A willful lemon law violation** / He had to pay for the repairs
- Fix: Keep every repair order and ask the maker in writing for a buyback or replacement.
- Source: `figueroa` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/8434997/figueroa-v-fca-us/) quote: "Figueroa took his truck back to the dealership another six to eight times complaining of overheating."
- Also: `civ-1793-2` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1793.2) quote: "(A) In the case of replacement, the manufacturer shall replace the buyer's vehicle with a new motor vehicle substantially identical to the vehicle replaced."

**lg29** (both, consumer). Figueroa had to sell his lemon truck and got about $3,000 more than he owed. FCA wanted that $3,000 taken off his award. Did the court agree?
- Choices: Yes, it is a fair credit / **No credit was given** / Half was credited
- Fix: Keep records of what you paid and any sale. The maker does not get credit for what you received selling it.
- Source: `figueroa` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/8434997/figueroa-v-fca-us/) quote: "Plaintiff was forced to sell the truck and received a little more than $3,000 than he owed on the loan to purchase the truck."

**lg30** (both, consumer). Justin Kiluk bought a certified pre-owned Mercedes for about $122,000. Mercedes said the lemon law only binds sellers of used cars. Who won?
- Choices: Mercedes, used cars are out / **Kiluk; its warranty bound it** / No one, it was settled
- Fix: Keep the warranty papers on a certified pre-owned car. A maker that issues a warranty can be held to it.
- Source: `kiluk` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4686676/kiluk-v-mercedes-benz-usa-llc/) quote: "Although the Song-Beverly Act generally binds only distributors and retail sellers in the sale of used goods, we conclude Mercedes Benz stepped into that role by issuing an express warranty on the sale of a used vehicle."

**lg31** (both, consumer). Wajeeh Mughrabi's Santa Ana market cashed 35 payroll checks that all bounced. The trial court gave him only the face amount. Was that right?
- Choices: Yes, the face amount of the checks / **No, treble damages are owed** / The bank fees only
- Fix: Send a written demand. If unpaid in 30 days, you can seek 3 times the check, at least $100 and at most $1,500 per check, unless the writer stopped payment over a good faith dispute.
- Source: `mughrabi` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2116109/mughrabi-v-suzuki/) quote: "3, ante.) Mughrabi's request for treble damages pursuant to the mandatory directive of section 1719 should have been granted."
- Also: `civ-1719` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1719) quote: "which shall not be less than one hundred dollars ($100) nor more than one thousand five hundred dollars ($1,500)"

**lg32** (both, consumer). Bill Toomey's company phoned people to sell $25 to $30 casino coupon books "worth hundreds." Buyers were not told they could cancel. What did the court order?
- Choices: Sales stood, with no refunds owed / **Refunds and $300,000 penalties** / A warning letter, no more
- Fix: Cancel in writing within the cancel window and keep proof. Report sellers who hide it to the Attorney General.
- Source: `toomey` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2121705/people-v-toomey/) quote: "$150,000 in penalties was assessed for violation of the preliminary injunction"
- Also: `civ-1689-6` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=CIV&sectionNum=1689.6) quote: "(4) The five-day right to cancel added by the act that amended paragraphs (1) and (2) shall apply to contracts entered into, or offers to purchase conveyed, on or after January 1, 2021."

**lg33** (both, consumer). The FTC said Amazon signed people up for Prime without clear consent and made it very hard to cancel. How did it end in 2025?
- Choices: It was dismissed in court / **A $2.5 billion settlement** / Amazon paid a $10,000 fine
- Fix: Cancel subscriptions online and screenshot it. Report traps to the FTC at ReportFraud.ftc.gov.
- Source: `ftc-amazon-prime` (https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2025/09/ftc-secures-historic-25-billion-settlement-against-amazon) quote: "Additionally, the settlement requires Amazon to stop their unlawful practices and make meaningful changes to the Prime enrollment and cancellation flows by: including a clear and conspicuous button for customers to decline Prime."

**lg34** (both, consumer). Kyle Kimoto's companies sold "credit cards" that were really debit offers, then enrolled buyers in "free trials" that started billing. What did the FTC win?
- Choices: The fine print covered him / **A court win against him** / A warning letter only
- Fix: Read before you click. Check your statement every month and dispute charges you never agreed to.
- Source: `ftc-grant-connect` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2718331/federal-trade-commission-v-grant-connect-llc/) quote: "SMITH, Circuit Judge: Kyle Kimoto (Kimoto) appeals from the district court's grant of summary judgment to the Federal Trade Commission (FTC), and its order permanently enjoining Kimoto from engaging in a variety of marketing tactics, and ordering him to pay restitution."

**lg35** (both, money). Omar Molina was found with a credit card in someone else's name. The owner had canceled it. Can holding a canceled card still be a crime?
- Choices: No, it is dead plastic / **Yes, it can still count** / If he used it, but not before
- Fix: Cut up old cards. Never hold or keep a card in someone else's name.
- Source: `molina-484e` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2257630/people-v-molina/) quote: "In the published portion of this opinion, we hold that possession of another's cancelled credit card constitutes possession of access card account information with respect to an access card validly issued to another."
- Also: `pen-484e` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=PEN&sectionNum=484e) quote: "(d) Every person who acquires or retains possession of access card account information with respect to an access card validly issued to another person, without the cardholder's or issuer's consent, with the intent to use it fraudulently, is guilty of grand theft."

**lg36** (both, money). Adedayo John directed others to open bank accounts and receive money sent by romance scam victims, many older. What happened to him?
- Choices: Probation, no prison time / **Seven years in prison** / A modest fine
- Fix: Never open accounts or receive money for someone you met online. Report the ask at ic3.gov.
- Source: `usss-john` (https://www.secretservice.gov/newsroom/releases/2024/06/leader-money-laundering-and-bank-fraud-ring-sentenced-seven-years-prison) quote: "JOHN directed other members of the conspiracy to open new bank accounts and/or to be ready to receive money that was going to be sent from others, and he assisted members with procuring fake passports and other identification documents in order to open fraudulent bank accounts."

**lg37** (both, money). In the Adedayo John case, how did victims get tricked into sending money to the ring's bank accounts?
- Choices: Fake lottery prize calls / **Fake romances online** / Robberies on the street
- Fix: If an online love interest asks for money or a transfer, stop and talk to family first.
- Source: `usss-john` (https://www.secretservice.gov/newsroom/releases/2024/06/leader-money-laundering-and-bank-fraud-ring-sentenced-seven-years-prison) quote: "Attorney Damian Williams said: \"The defendants in this case have acknowledged their roles as part of a criminal enterprise that not only defrauded businesses by assuming the online identities of legitimate entities, but also preyed on elderly people, deceiving victims into sending money in phony romance scams."

**lg38** (both, money). Thieves made unauthorized withdrawals from Margaretha Widjaja's Chase account, starting with a transfer under $2, while she was overseas. How fast must a customer report to keep EFTA protection?
- Choices: Within one full year / **60 days after the statement** / Within 24 hours of the theft
- Fix: Read every statement. Report any odd charge, even $2, right away and in writing.
- Source: `widjaja` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/5307933/margaretha-widjaja-v-jpmorgan-chase-bank-na/) quote: "(2) the consumer fails to report the unauthorized transfer to her bank within 60 days after the statement is sent to her;"
- Also: `usc-15-1693g` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/1693g) quote: "(d)Restriction on liability Nothing in this section imposes liability upon a consumer for an unauthorized electronic fund transfer in excess of his liability for such a transfer under other applicable law or under any agreement with the consumer's financial institution."

**lg39** (both, money). John Gorman disputed a credit card charge for a satellite TV system he was unhappy with. What must the card bank do after a dispute through the credit bureaus?
- Choices: It can ignore the dispute / **A reasonable investigation** / Erase the whole debt
- Fix: Dispute in writing with both the card issuer and the credit bureaus, and keep copies.
- Source: `gorman` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/3054308/gorman-v-wolpoff-abramson-llp/) quote: "a furnisher's obligation to conduct a reasonable investigation under § 1681s-2(b)(l)(A) arises when it receives a notice of dispute from a CRA"

**lg40** (both, money). Maria Hernandez's debt passed to a second collector, which never sent its own validation notice. Did it have to?
- Choices: No, the first one did / **Yes, all collectors must** / When she asked, yes
- Fix: Wait for each collector's notice and dispute in writing within 30 days if anything looks wrong.
- Source: `hernandez-wzp` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4240209/maria-hernandez-v-williams-zinman-parham-pc/) quote: "According to WZP, under this definition, it was not obligated to send a validation notice because, as the second collector to attempt to collect the debt, it did not send Hernandez the very first (i.e., \"the initial\") communication about the debt."
- Also: `usc-15-1692g` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/1692g) quote: "(3)a statement that unless the consumer, within thirty days after receipt of the notice, disputes the validity of the debt, or any portion thereof, the debt will be assumed to be valid by the debt collector;"

**lg41** (both, tax). Frank Ahlgren sold about $3.7 million in bitcoin in 2017 to buy a house and claimed fake high purchase prices. What happened?
- Choices: The charges were dropped / **Two years in prison** / A warning letter from the IRS
- Fix: Report every crypto sale and keep records of what you paid.
- Source: `doj-ahlgren` (https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/early-bitcoin-investor-sentenced-filing-tax-returns-falsely-reported-his-cryptocurrency) quote: "In October 2017, Ahlgren sold approximately 640 bitcoins trading at approximately $5,807.53 per bitcoin for a total of $3.7 million, and used the proceeds to purchase a house in Park City, Utah."
- Also: `irs-crypto` (https://www.irs.gov/filing/digital-assets) quote: "Income from digital assets is taxable."

**lg42** (both, tax). For 2018 and 2019, Ahlgren moved bitcoin through mixers and traded for cash in person. Did hiding it work?
- Choices: Yes, mixers hide everything / **No, he was caught** / Temporarily, for one year
- Fix: Do not try to hide crypto gains. Report gains and losses on your return.
- Source: `doj-ahlgren` (https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/early-bitcoin-investor-sentenced-filing-tax-returns-falsely-reported-his-cryptocurrency) quote: "In 2018 and 2019, Ahlgren sold bitcoins for more than $650,000, and did not report these sales at all on his 2018 and 2019 tax returns."

**lg43** (both, tax). David and Christine Gates tore down their old Santa Barbara house, built a new one, never lived in it, then sold. Could they exclude $500,000 of gain?
- Choices: Yes, the land was theirs / **No, the exclusion failed** / Half of it, yes
- Fix: Before you sell, check that you owned and lived in that home 2 of the last 5 years.
- Source: `gates` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4560935/gates-v-commissioner/) quote: "Respondent contends that petitioners did not sell property they had owned and used as their principal residence for the required statutory period because they never occupied the new house as their principal residence before they sold it."
- Also: `usc-26-121` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/121) quote: "(5)Exclusion of gain allocated to nonqualified use (A)In general Subsection (a) shall not apply to so much of the gain from the sale or exchange of property as is allocated to periods of nonqualified use."

**lg44** (both, tax). The Engdahls ran a saddlebred horse ranch for years at a loss and did most of the work. The IRS called it a hobby. Who won?
- Choices: The IRS, losses mean hobby / **The Engdahls; a real business** / No one, it settled
- Fix: Run a side venture like a business: books, a plan, and real effort. That is what courts look at.
- Source: `engdahl` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/4705020/engdahl-v-commissioner/) quote: "Held, on the facts, the horse-breeding operation was an activity engaged in for profit under sec."
- Also: `irs-hobby` (https://www.irs.gov/newsroom/know-the-difference-between-a-hobby-and-a-business) quote: "https://www.irs.gov/newsroom/know-the-difference-between-a-hobby-and-a-business FS-2022-38, October 2022 Most people take up hobbies for personal enjoyment, not to make a profit."

**lg45** (both, insurance). Michael Egan hurt his back at work. Mutual of Omaha cut off his disability pay without having a doctor examine him or asking his own doctors. What did the court say?
- Choices: Insurers deny whenever / **That broke good faith** / Entirely the agent's decision
- Fix: Ask for the denial reason in writing and for the claim file. A denial with no real look can be bad faith.
- Source: `egan` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1217902/egan-v-mutual-of-omaha-insurance/) quote: "The trial court, in directing a verdict against defendants, ruled as a matter of law that defendants' failure to have plaintiff examined by a doctor of their choice or to consult with plaintiff's treating physicians and surgeon violated the covenant of good faith and fair dealing."

**lg46** (both, insurance). Egan won at trial against Mutual of Omaha and two of its claims staff. What did the California Supreme Court keep?
- Choices: The whole verdict, as is / **Compensatory damages vs. Mutual** / The jury verdict was voided
- Fix: Keep every letter and note. Appeals can change parts of a verdict, so a strong record matters.
- Source: `egan` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1217902/egan-v-mutual-of-omaha-insurance/) quote: "We conclude *815the judgment should be affirmed insofar as it awards compensatory damages against defendant Mutual of Omaha Insurance Company (Mutual) but reversed in all other respects."

**lg47** (both, insurance). Jerome Gruenberg's Brass Rail lounge burned. The adjuster told arson police he was over-insured; the arson charges were dismissed. Insurers still denied. Could he sue for bad faith?
- Choices: No, just the policy amount / **Yes, bad faith is a tort** / He can sue the arson unit
- Fix: Keep notes of every call and letter. Unfair handling can be a bad faith tort with extra damages.
- Source: `gruenberg` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1117033/gruenberg-v-aetna-insurance/) quote: "In response the Fletcher court observed: \"An insurer owes to its insured an implied-in-law duty of good faith and fair dealing that it will do nothing to deprive the insured of the benefits of the policy."

**lg48** (both, insurance). In Gruenberg, the insurers demanded an exam under oath while arson charges were pending. His lawyer asked to wait. What did the insurers do?
- Choices: Waited for the charges to end / **Denied for not appearing** / Paid in full at once
- Fix: Talk to a lawyer before an exam under oath, and ask in writing for a delay if charges are pending.
- Source: `gruenberg` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1117033/gruenberg-v-aetna-insurance/) quote: "Ricketts refused the request and warned that failure to appear for the examination would void coverage under the policies."

**lg49** (both, insurance). Owners found a big foundation crack in 1985 and filed a claim. Prudential took over a year to investigate. They sued 20 months after the claim. Too late?
- Choices: Yes, a year is a year / **No, the clock was paused** / Late, unless the crack was new
- Fix: Write down when you gave notice and when the denial came. The clock pauses in between.
- Source: `prudential-lmi` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1258604/prudential-lmi-commercial-insurance-v-superior-court/) quote: "If it is found that plaintiffs' delayed discovery of the loss was reasonable, then the rule of equitable tolling would operate to toll the one-year suit provision from the date the insured filed a timely notice of loss to Prudential's formal denial of coverage."
- Also: `ins-2071` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=INS&sectionNum=2071) quote: "unless commenced within 12 months next after inception of the loss"

**lg50** (both, insurance). In Prudential-LMI, the damage built up slowly over years. When does the one-year clock first start for a slowly growing loss?
- Choices: The day the policy began / **When damage is appreciable** / The day of the denial letter
- Fix: Report damage as soon as you notice it, and keep the date in writing.
- Source: `prudential-lmi` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1258604/prudential-lmi-commercial-insurance-v-superior-court/) quote: "we hold that the one-year suit provision begins to run on the date of inception of the loss, defined as that point in time when appreciable damage occurs and is or should be known to the insured"

**lg51** (both, insurance). In the 2007 Hailey case, Blue Shield could rescind a plan if it had checked the health form. Newer laws replaced that rule. Today, when may a plan rescind?
- Choices: If the form has any mistake / **Only for fraud or lies** / After a late premium payment
- Fix: Today a plan may rescind only for fraud or an intentional lie about a material fact, with 30 days written notice and a right to appeal. Plans can no longer turn you down or charge more for past health.
- Source: `hsc-1389-21` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=HSC&sectionNum=1389.21) quote: "made an intentional misrepresentation of material fact"
- Also: `usc-42-300gg-12` (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/42/300gg-12) quote: "makes an intentional misrepresentation of material fact"
- Also: `hailey` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2244781/hailey-v-california-physicians-service/) quote: "Blue Shield"
- Also: `hsc-1389-3` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=HSC&sectionNum=1389.3) quote: "No health care service plan shall engage in the practice of postclaims underwriting"
- Also: `hc-preexisting` (https://www.healthcare.gov/coverage/pre-existing-conditions/) quote: "No insurance plan can reject you, charge you more"

**lg52** (both, insurance). After Blue Shield rescinded their plan, a trial court, on summary judgment, ordered the Haileys to repay $104,194.12. What did the Court of Appeal do?
- Choices: Raised it to add fees / **It reversed the judgment** / Upheld the award in full
- Fix: A trial loss can be undone on appeal. And under today's rules, ask the state regulator to review any rescission.
- Source: `hailey` (https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/2244781/hailey-v-california-physicians-service/) quote: "The judgment is reversed"
- Also: `hsc-1389-21` (https://leginfo.legislature.ca.gov/faces/codes_displaySection.xhtml?lawCode=HSC&sectionNum=1389.21) quote: "at least 30 days prior to the effective date of the rescission"

## Field Survival: Water, Food and Knots (`survive`)

**sv01** (both, water) img: water-treat.svg. What is the best way to kill germs in water?
- Choices: Set it in sun / **Boil it well** / Add some salt
- Fix: Boiling is the best way to kill germs in water.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Boiling is the best way to kill germs in water."

**sv02** (both, water) img: water-treat.svg. How long should clear water boil to be safe at normal elevation?
- Choices: Boil ten minutes straight / **Rolling boil, 1 minute** / Until it just steams
- Fix: Bring clear water to a rolling boil for 1 minute. Above 6,500 feet, boil 3 minutes.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Bring clear water to a rolling boil for 1 minute (at elevations above 6,500 feet, boil for 3 minutes)."

**sv03** (both, water) img: water-treat.svg. The water is cloudy. What do you do before boiling or treating it?
- Choices: Add extra chlorine first / **Filter through clean cloth** / Stir it well
- Fix: Filter it through a clean cloth, paper towel or coffee filter, or let it settle.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "If the water is cloudy, first filter it through a clean cloth, paper towel, or coffee filter."

**sv04** (both, water). Boiled water tastes flat. How can you improve the taste?
- Choices: Add a little sugar / **Pour back and forth** / Stir bleach in, taste
- Fix: Pour it between containers and let it stand a few hours, or add a pinch of salt per quart.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Pouring it from one container to another and then letting it stand for a few hours"

**sv05** (both, water). Your flood water has fuel in it. Will boiling make it safe?
- Choices: Boiling fixes absolutely anything / **Some can't be safe** / Bleach fixes it all
- Fix: Boiling or disinfecting can't make water with fuel or chemicals safe.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "You cannot make water containing fuel, toxic chemicals, or radioactive materials safe by boiling or disinfecting it."

**sv06** (adult, water). The power is out. Can you drink water from the home boiler or radiators?
- Choices: It's perfectly clean / **It can sicken you** / Drinkable once cooled down
- Fix: Never use water from radiators or boilers for drinking, cooking or washing.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Never use water from radiators or boilers that are part of your home heating system for drinking, cooking, or hygiene."

**sv07** (both, water). After treating water with bleach, how long should it stand?
- Choices: About 5 minutes / **About 30 minutes, minimum** / Roughly 2 hours, max
- Fix: Let the water stand for at least 30 minutes before you drink it.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Let the water stand for at least 30 minutes before you drink it."

**sv08** (adult, water). The water is cloudy or very cold. How much bleach do you use?
- Choices: Half the usual amount / **Double the usual amount** / Exactly the usual amount
- Fix: For cloudy, colored or very cold water, double the bleach amount.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "If the water is cloudy, murky, colored, or very cold, add double the amount of bleach listed below."

**sv09** (both, water). What kind of bleach is right for treating water?
- Choices: Scented, nicer taste / **Plain, unscented bleach** / Gel, splash-less bleach
- Fix: Use unscented household chlorine bleach.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Use unscented household chlorine bleach"

**sv10** (both, water). Do chemical tablets kill every germ, like boiling does?
- Choices: They kill every germ / **May miss parasites** / They are better than boiling
- Fix: Disinfectants kill viruses and bacteria but may miss parasites like Cryptosporidium.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Chemical disinfectants kill viruses and bacteria, but may not kill parasites."

**sv11** (adult, water). Who should avoid water treated with iodine?
- Choices: Children under eighteen / **Pregnant, thyroid issues** / Seniors over sixty-five
- Fix: Avoid iodine water if pregnant, with thyroid problems or iodine allergy. No one should use it for more than a few weeks.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "If you are pregnant, have thyroid problems, or have a hypersensitivity to iodine, avoid drinking water disinfected with iodine."

**sv12** (both, water). Does a small hiking water filter remove all germs?
- Choices: It removes all germs / **Most miss viruses** / It works on muddy water
- Fix: Portable filters don't remove all germs. Add a disinfectant after filtering.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Portable water filters do not remove all types of germs from water."

**sv13** (ya, water). Does UV light treat cloudy water well?
- Choices: Works in any water / **Particles block the light** / Better in cloudy, murky water
- Fix: UV light doesn't work well in cloudy water, since particles block germs from the light.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "UV light does not work well in cloudy water because small particles can block germs from the light."

**sv14** (ya, water). Using clear bottles and sunlight to treat water, how long do they lie in the sun?
- Choices: Roughly 30 minutes / **About 6 sunny hours** / Merely 1 hour
- Fix: Lay bottles on their side in the sun for 6 hours if sunny, 2 days if cloudy.
- Source: `cdc-water` (https://www.cdc.gov/water-emergency/about/) quote: "Lay the bottles down on their side and in the sun for 6 hours (if sunny) or 2 days (if cloudy)."

**sv15** (both, water). You must pick between a running stream and a still pond. Which is better?
- Choices: The still, clear pond / **The running stream water** / Either one, left untreated
- Fix: Choose running water, but still treat it.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "If you have to choose between running water and stagnate water, choose running water but make sure it is also appropriately treated."

**sv16** (both, water). You are thirsty in the snow. Should you eat snow?
- Choices: Eat it; it's water / **Melt it, then treat** / Eat fresh, clean snow
- Fix: Don't eat snow. Melt it and purify it. Eating it chills you and wastes energy.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Do not eat snow."

**sv17** (both, water). You are stranded at the beach. Can you drink a little seawater?
- Choices: A little sip is okay / **None, not one sip** / Okay if mixed with rain
- Fix: Don't drink saltwater, even a small amount.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Do not drink saltwater, even a small amount."

**sv18** (both, water). Can drinking urine help you survive without water?
- Choices: It helps in a pinch / **Drinking it harms** / Drinkable when very clear
- Fix: Don't drink urine or alcohol.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Do not drink urine or alcoholic beverages."

**sv19** (both, water). Should you treat clear surface water from a lake or creek?
- Choices: Clear water is safe / **Treat it anyway, still** / Big rains make it unsafe
- Fix: Treat all surface water as dirty with human and animal waste.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Consider all surface water to be contaminated with human and animal waste"

**sv20** (both, water). With little activity at 68°F, how much water does a person need a day?
- Choices: Merely 1 cup / **About 2 to 3 quarts** / Around 2 gallons
- Fix: Plan on 2 to 3 quarts a day at rest, more in heat.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "personnel will normally require 2 to 3 quarts of water a day"

**sv21** (ya, water). In cold weather, how should you store a water jug?
- Choices: Upright, cap up top / **Upside down, cap low** / Lying on its side, flat
- Fix: Store it upside down. Water freezes top first, so the opening stays clear.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Water containers in a cold weather environment should be stores upside down"

**sv22** (ya, water). Is snow or ice cleaner than the water it came from?
- Choices: Freezing purifies it / **No purer than before** / Ice is always pure
- Fix: Snow and ice are no purer than the water they came from.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Snow or ice are no purer than the water from which they come."

**sv23** (both, food). You find wild mushrooms. Are they a good survival food?
- Choices: Most are quite tasty / **Avoid them all** / Cooked ones work fine
- Fix: Avoid mushrooms. There is no field test, and some kill with no antidote.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Avoid mushrooms and fungi; they are not technically plants."

**sv24** (both, food). A wild plant has milky sap. Is it likely safe?
- Choices: Milky means it's sweet / **It may be toxic** / Edible once it's boiled
- Fix: Milky sap is a sign of a poisonous plant.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Plants with a milky sap; this is an indicator of a poisonous plant."

**sv27** (ya, food). A plant's leaves smell like almonds. Good sign?
- Choices: Sweet means edible / **Possible poison, beware** / Okay, if wilted first
- Fix: An almond scent can mean cyanide compounds. Avoid it.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Avoid any weed, leaves, or seeds with an almond like scent"

**sv28** (ya, food). A wild plant grows in groups of three leaves. Eat it?
- Choices: Three leaves bring luck / **Stay away from it** / Eat it whenever green
- Fix: Avoid wild plants with a three-leafed growth pattern.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "A three-leafed growth pattern."

**sv29** (ya, food). Which insects should you avoid eating?
- Choices: Plain brown crickets, cooked / **Hairy or bright ones** / Plain black ants, cooked
- Fix: Avoid insects that bite or sting, and hairy or brightly colored ones.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Insects to avoid include all adults that bite or sting and hairy or brightly colored insects."

**sv30** (ya, food). Many insects have more protein than beef. About how much?
- Choices: About 5 percent / **About 65 to 80%** / Close to 20 percent
- Fix: Many insects have 65 to 80 percent protein, compared to about 20 percent for beef.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Many insects provide 65 to 80 percent protein compared to only 20 percent for beef."

**sv31** (both, food). Should you cook wild game, freshwater fish and clams fully?
- Choices: Rare is okay for fish / **Fully cook all of it** / Raw is best for clams
- Fix: Fully cook wild game, freshwater fish, clams, mussels and snails to kill parasites.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Fully cook wild game, large insects, freshwater fish, clams, mussels, snails and crawfish to kill any internal parasites."

**sv32** (adult, food). Acorns taste very bitter. What helps?
- Choices: Eat them raw, quickly / **Boil in changing water** / Throw every one out
- Fix: Boiling in several changes of water usually removes the bitter tannins.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Boiling them in several changes of water will usually remove these bitter properties."

**sv33** (adult, food). Fruit is starting to mold. Is it safe to eat around the spot?
- Choices: Cut the spot off / **Pass on moldy fruit** / Cook it, then eat
- Fix: Don't eat fruit starting to spoil or show mildew or fungus. Some fungal toxins are deadly.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "do not eat any fruit starting to spoil or showing signs of mildew or fungus."

**sv34** (adult, food). You found a safe wild food. How much should you eat at first?
- Choices: As much as possible / **A little at a time** / Only the roots, nothing
- Fix: Even safe plant food can cause cramps on an empty stomach. Eat in moderation.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Even after testing plant food and finding it safe, eat it in moderation."

**sv35** (adult, food). How do you get worms ready to eat in a pinch?
- Choices: Fry them in oil / **Soak in clean water** / Dry in sun
- Fix: Put them in clean water for 15 minutes to purge, then they can be eaten.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "In 15 minutes, the worms will purge themselves and wash themselves out"

**sv36** (adult, food). You clean game in camp. How long should a bleach sanitizer sit on surfaces?
- Choices: About 10 seconds / **About ten full minutes** / Nearly 1 whole hour
- Fix: Leave the sanitizer on surfaces for a full ten minutes before rinsing.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Leave the solution on the surfaces for a full tem minutes before rinsing off."

**sv37** (both, shelter). How thick should a bed of leaves or boughs be under you?
- Choices: Roughly 1 inch deep / **About 12 inches minimum** / About 3 inches deep
- Fix: Make natural ground insulation at least 12 inches thick. It packs down.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "make the insulation a minimum of 12 inches thick."

**sv38** (both, shelter). Is it smart to build a fire inside a crashed car or plane you use as shelter?
- Choices: Smart; it's sheltered / **Unwise; fuel may burn** / Smart, in the trunk
- Fix: Don't build a fire in or near wreckage. Spilled fuel can ignite.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "It is unwise to build a fire in or near an aircraft or vehicle wreckage"

**sv39** (both, shelter). You use a candle in a snow shelter. Why leave a vent hole open?
- Choices: For a nicer view / **Candles make CO gas** / For moonlight to shine
- Fix: Candles, fuel gel and small lamps make carbon monoxide. Keep vents open.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Candles, fuel gel, or small oil lamps produce carbon monoxide."

**sv40** (adult, shelter). About how far should a fire be from a shelter opening?
- Choices: About 20 feet away / **About 3 feet away** / Up against the door
- Fix: The best fire-to-shelter distance is about 3 feet, with the opening sideways to the wind.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The best fireto-shelter distance is about 3 feet."

**sv41** (both, knots). You need to tie two ropes of the same size together. Which knot?
- Choices: A granny knot / **A square knot** / A slip knot
- Fix: A square knot joins two ropes of equal size.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The square knot is used for tying two ropes of equal diameter together to prevent slippage"

**sv42** (both, knots). Your square knot's ends don't lie flat beside their own rope. What did you tie?
- Choices: A bowline loop / **A granny knot** / A sheet bend
- Fix: That's a granny knot. It slips under strain, so don't use it.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "the knot is called a "granny knot." Because it will slip under the strain, a granny knot should not be used."

**sv43** (both, knots). A "thief knot" looks like a square knot. Is it safe to use?
- Choices: It holds as well / **It slips under load** / It is much stronger
- Fix: A thief knot slips under strain and is hard to untie.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "This knot is called a "thief" knot. It will slip under the strain and is difficult to untie."

**sv44** (both, knots). You must join a thin rope to a thick one. Which knot?
- Choices: A square knot / **A sheet bend** / A granny knot
- Fix: A sheet bend joins ropes of different sizes.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "limited to tying together two dry ropes of unequal size."

**sv45** (adult, knots). Joining wet ropes for a heavy load: single or double sheet bend?
- Choices: Single works best / **Only the double** / Either is same
- Fix: The double sheet bend is better for wet ropes and won't slip under heavy loads.
