- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The double sheet bend works better than the single sheet bend for joining ropes of equal or unequal diameter, joining wet ropes"

**sv46** (both, knots). You need a loop at the end of a rope that won't slip and is easy to untie. Which knot?
- Choices: A granny knot / **A bowline knot** / A slip knot
- Fix: The bowline forms a loop at the end of a rope and is easy to untie.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The bowline is a useful knot for forming a loop in the end of a rope. It is also easy to untie."

**sv47** (both, knots). You need to tie a line to a post or tent stake. Which knot works well?
- Choices: A square knot / **A clove hitch** / A granny knot
- Fix: A clove hitch fastens a rope to a timber, pipe or post.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "A clove hitch is used to fasten a rope to a timber, pipe, or post."

**sv48** (both, knots). Does a plain overhand knot weaken a rope?
- Choices: It makes rope stronger / **It cuts strength 55%** / It has no effect
- Fix: An overhand knot alone cuts a straight rope's strength by 55 percent.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "This knot alone will reduce the strength of a straight rope by 55 percent."

**sv49** (both, knots). What knot keeps a rope end from slipping through a loop?
- Choices: A clove hitch knot / **A figure-eight loop** / A sliding loop knot
- Fix: A figure-eight knot at the end keeps it from slipping through a loop.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "A figure-eight knot is used in the end of a rope to prevent the ends from slipping through a fastening"

**sv50** (ya, knots). What's a quick way to tie a rope to a pole?
- Choices: A square knot / **Two half hitches** / A doubled granny
- Fix: Two half hitches are a quick way to tie a rope to a pole.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "A quick method for tying a rope to a timber or pole is to use two half hitches."

**sv51** (ya, knots). You need a strong anchor knot to a tree that won't jam. Which one?
- Choices: A plain granny knot / **Turn, two half hitches** / A loose slip knot
- Fix: A round turn and two half hitches is the main anchor knot.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The round turn and two half hitches (seen in figure A-3) is the main anchor knot"

**sv52** (ya, knots). You double a rope's thickness. How much stronger is it?
- Choices: Twice as strong / **Four times stronger** / Practically the same
- Fix: Doubling the diameter makes rope about four times as strong.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Doubling the diameter quadruples the tensile strength"

**sv53** (ya, knots). How should you store a nylon rope?
- Choices: Out in direct sun / **Clean and shaded** / Wet, bagged up tight
- Fix: Keep manmade ropes clean and out of the sun to protect them from UV damage.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Manmade fiber ropes are good for tying knots but should still be kept clean and out of the sun"

**sv54** (adult, knots). Which knot works as a sling to lift an unconscious person?
- Choices: A square knot / **A French bowline** / A trucker's hitch
- Fix: The French bowline can be a sling. The weight keeps both loops tight.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "The French bowline is sometimes used as a sling for lifting people."

**sv66** (both, food). Should you eat beans and peas you find growing wild?
- Choices: Edible, like store beans / **Pass on wild legumes** / Okay, when eaten raw
- Fix: Avoid the whole wild legume family. They can absorb minerals and upset the gut.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "All of the legume family (beans and peas)."

**sv67** (both, food). A plant has umbrella-shaped flowers, like wild carrot. Safe to forage?
- Choices: Edible, like carrots / **Hemlock is kin** / Tasty, root white
- Fix: Avoid umbrella-shaped flowers. Water hemlock, one of the deadliest plants, is in this family.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "One of the most poisonous plants, water hemlock, is also a member of this family."

**sv68** (both, food). Should you consider plants with shiny leaves safe?
- Choices: Shiny means it's fresh / **Treat as poison, always** / Glossy means it's ripe
- Fix: Consider plants with shiny leaves as poisonous.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Consider plants with shiny leaves as poisonous."

**sv69** (both, water). Water sits in a low dip in the ground. Is it a good source?
- Choices: Clean after rain / **Often polluted water** / Best of all choices
- Fix: Don't drink water from natural dips. It's still and often polluted.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "Do not drink water found in natural depressions."

**sv70** (both, water). In high heat, how much water can you lose to sweat?
- Choices: About 1 cup hourly / **Up to 3.5 quarts hourly** / Around 1 quart daily, roughly
- Fix: In high heat, sweat loss can reach 3.5 quarts per hour.
- Source: `atp-3-50-21` (https://armypubs.army.mil/epubs/DR_pubs/DR_a/pdf/web/ARN12086_ATP%203-50x21%20FINAL%20WEB%202.pdf) quote: "water loss from sweat increases to as much as 3.5 quarts per hour."

**sv71** (both, food). On a hike, your kids find wild berries that look like store berries. Can they eat some?
- Choices: Yes, if they look like store ones / **Only once an expert IDs them** / No, unless they are red ones
- Fix: Never eat wild berries or plants you can't name for certain. Kids should always check with an adult first.
- Source: `poison-poke` (https://www.poison.org/articles/pokeberries-and-grapes-look-alike) quote: "Children should be taught never to eat wild berries unless they first check with a knowledgeable adult." (Poison Control plant page: "only eat wild plants if you know FOR CERTAIN what they are")

**sv72** (both, food). Someone ate a wild plant you can't name. What do you do?
- Choices: Wait and see if they feel sick / **Phone Poison Control for help** / Make them throw it all up
- Fix: Call Poison Control at 1-800-222-1222, or use their online tool. Don't guess.
- Source: `poison-plant` (https://www.poison.org/articles/plant) quote: "If someone eats a poisonous plant, use the web POISON CONTROL online tool to get expert help, or call your poison center at 1-800-222-1222." "Expert help depends on accurate plant identification."

**sv73** (both, food). A plant looks like wild carrot. Safe to eat on a camping trip?
- Choices: Yes, wild carrot is safe to eat / **Only if you know it for certain** / No, unless you cook it first
- Fix: Only eat a wild plant if you know for certain what it is. People have died mistaking hemlock for wild carrot.
- Source: `poison-plant` (https://www.poison.org/articles/plant) quote: "only eat wild plants if you know FOR CERTAIN what they are. People have died after mistaking hemlock for wild carrots."

**sv74** (both, food). Is a plant that is not poisonous always safe for a toddler to chew?
- Choices: Yes, it is totally safe to chew / **No, it could still choke them** / No, unless it has thorns on it
- Fix: Even plants that are not poisonous can make someone choke. Watch young children closely outdoors.
- Source: `poison-plant` (https://www.poison.org/articles/plant) quote: "Even non-poisonous plants can cause choking if someone tries to eat them." "Watch young children carefully when they play outdoors."

**sv75** (both, food). A child took a bite of a wild mushroom from the yard. What now?
- Choices: Wait, since most are harmless / **Phone Poison Control now** / Have them drink milk, then wait
- Fix: Call your poison center right away if anyone eats any part of a wild mushroom.
- Source: `poison-plant` (https://www.poison.org/articles/plant) quote: "Call your poison center right away if anyone eats any part of a mushroom picked from a yard or the woods."

**sv76** (both, traps). In California, can you set traps or snares for fun or for fur?
- Choices: Yes, anywhere, with a license / **Only for licensed pest control** / No, unless the traps are tagged
- Fix: California trapping licenses are issued only for pest control. Check your state wildlife agency before you set anything.
- Source: `cdfw-trap` (https://wildlife.ca.gov/Licensing/Trapping) quote: Trapping License, Resident: "Issued to a person who traps fur-bearing or nongame mammals for pest control purposes." (Also Fish and Game Code 3003.1: body-gripping traps, including snares, are banned for recreation or commercial fur.)

**sv77** (both, traps). A survival video shows how to set snares. Should you practice on wild animals?
- Choices: Yes, practice makes it perfect / **Learn it, set none** / No, unless on public land
- Fix: Snaring is tightly limited by law. Treat it as an emergency-only skill, and check CDFW rules.
- Source: `cdfw-trap` (https://wildlife.ca.gov/Licensing/Trapping) quote: Trapping License, Resident: "Issued to a person who traps fur-bearing or nongame mammals for pest control purposes." (Also Fish and Game Code 3003.1: body-gripping traps, including snares, are banned for recreation or commercial fur.)

## Field Care: Triage and Scene (`triage`)

**tr01** (both, triage). A bus crash leaves many hurt. You call out, "If you can walk, come to me." Who do you check last?
- Choices: The quiet ones lying still / **The ones who walked over** / The ones waving at you
- Fix: The walkers go to a safe spot and get checked last.
- Source: `chemm-salt` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage) quote: "Patients who are able to walk should be directed to a safe area and assessed last."

**tr02** (both, triage) img: triage-tags.svg. In a crowd of hurt people, who do you check first?
- Choices: The loudest one, who screams most / **Those lying still or bleeding badly** / Whoever can walk to you first
- Fix: Check first those who are not moving or have a clear life threat, like heavy bleeding.
- Source: `chemm-salt` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage) quote: "Patients who are not moving or have an obvious life threat should be assessed first."

**tr03** (both, triage). How long should each person's quick check take at a mass crash?
- Choices: About 10 minutes each, fully / **Less than 1 minute each** / Five minutes per person
- Fix: Each quick check should take less than one minute.
- Source: `chemm-salt` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage) quote: "Individual patient assessments should take less than one minute"

**tr04** (both, triage) img: triage-tags.svg. What does a red triage tag mean?
- Choices: Dead, or sadly past all help / **Life threat, but likely to live** / Minor hurts; can wait a while
- Fix: Red means immediate: badly hurt, but with a good chance to live with fast care.
- Source: `chemm-start` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage) quote: "Immediate, Red Tag: Patient has severe or life-threatening injuries but a high potential for survival"

**tr05** (both, triage) img: triage-tags.svg. What does a yellow triage tag mean?
- Choices: Needs care this very minute / **Serious, but care can wait** / Fine; may go home now
- Fix: Yellow means delayed: serious, but not about to die in the next hours.
- Source: `chemm-start` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage) quote: "Delayed, Yellow Tag: Patient has serious but not immediately life-threatening injuries."

**tr06** (both, triage) img: triage-tags.svg. Someone with a green tag can do what?
- Choices: Nothing; they must lie still / **Help with their own care** / Go first in the ambulance
- Fix: Green means minor. They may help in their own care and can wait.
- Source: `chemm-start` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage) quote: "Patient may be able to assist in their own care and can wait for treatment/transportation until more serious patients are handled."

**tr07** (both, triage) img: triage-tags.svg. In SALT triage, which tag means "not expected to survive"?
- Choices: A black tag, every time / **A grey tag, in SALT** / A blue tag, used rarely
- Fix: SALT uses grey for expectant and black for dead.
- Source: `chemm-salt` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage) quote: "Expectant, Grey Tag: Patient is not expected to survive"

**tr08** (both, triage). In SALT, a black tag means what?
- Choices: Hurt, but can wait hours / **Dead, or not breathing** / Needs care first, fast
- Fix: Black is for those who are not breathing or have injuries no one can live through.
- Source: `chemm-salt` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage) quote: "Dead, Black Tag: Patient who is not breathing or who has injuries incompatible with life."

**tr09** (both, triage). START triage checks the mind in a quick way. How?
- Choices: Ask them the date and year / **See if they obey commands** / Ask them to count to 100
- Fix: START checks if the person can follow simple commands.
- Source: `chemm-start` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage) quote: "Mental status, as assessed by ability to obey commands"

**tr10** (both, triage). You press a fingernail and let go. The pink color is slow to come back. How slow is too slow?
- Choices: Over 10 seconds is slow / **Over 2 seconds is slow** / Over 5 seconds is slow
- Fix: If the color takes more than 2 seconds, refill is delayed.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "If it takes more than 2 seconds for the color to return, capillary refill is considered delayed."

**tr12** (both, triage). START triage is meant for people who look older than what age?
- Choices: Only those over 18 / **Those older than 8** / Every age, even babies
- Fix: START is for people who look older than 8. Kids have their own tool.
- Source: `chemm-start` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage) quote: "assigns patients who appear to be older than 8 years old"

**tr13** (both, triage). During a SALT check, one person is bleeding hard from a leg. What do you do before you move on?
- Choices: Tag them and keep going fast / **Stop the bleeding with a tourniquet** / Splint the leg, then go on
- Fix: SALT says to do quick lifesaving steps, like a tourniquet for heavy bleeding.
- Source: `chemm-salt` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/salt-triage) quote: "controlling major hemorrhage with a tourniquet"

**tr14** (both, triage). An hour ago you tagged a man yellow. Now he is pale and fast breathing. What now?
- Choices: A tag, once given, stays put / **Check again; tags can change** / Wait, since yellow means later
- Fix: Triage keeps changing. Check people again and change the tag when needed.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Triage is a dynamic process"

**tr15** (both, triage). What is the main goal of triage?
- Choices: Help the worst hurt first, always / **Save the most people you can** / Treat people in arrival order
- Fix: Triage tries to save the most people with the time and supplies you have.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "triage is based on the concept of saving the maximum number of personnel possible"

**tr16** (both, scene). The 911 caller said "she just fainted." You get there. Do you trust that report?
- Choices: Trust it; the caller saw it / **Keep an open mind** / Treat it as a simple faint
- Fix: Do not let what you heard before you got there make up your mind.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not let information you received before your arrival form your complete conclusion"

**tr17** (both, scene). In the first fast check of a hurt person, when do you fix a problem you find?
- Choices: After the full exam is done / **The moment you find it** / Once the ambulance gets there
- Fix: In the first check, fix each big problem right when you find it.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "As each major problem is detected, it is treated immediately, before moving on to the next."

**tr18** (both, scene). In the ABCDE first check, what does the A stand for?
- Choices: Arms and legs, for breaks / **Airway, so air can get in** / Alert, or asleep, or out cold
- Fix: The ABCDEs are airway, breathing, circulation, disability and expose.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "airway, breathing, circulation, disability, and expose."

**tr19** (both, scene). About how fast should the ABCDE check and the transport choice be done?
- Choices: Within 1 hour, if possible / **Within 10 minutes, if possible** / Within 2 hours, if possible
- Fix: Ideally the first check, status and transport choice take 10 minutes or less.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Ideally, the ABCDE steps, status, and transport decision should be completed within 10 minutes of your arrival on the scene."

**tr20** (both, scene). If the heart stops, how soon can the brain start to be harmed for good?
- Choices: After about 1 hour / **In 4 to 6 minutes** / After about 30 minutes
- Fix: Lasting brain harm can start in 4 to 6 minutes.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Irreversible changes will begin to occur in the brain in 4 to 6 minutes"

**tr21** (both, scene). A hurt man is not talking much and wears a thick coat. Do you look under it?
- Choices: Leave it on; it keeps him warm / **Look under it, then cover him** / Only if he says it hurts there
- Fix: You can't treat what you have not found. Look, then keep them warm and covered.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "You cannot treat conditions you have not discovered."

**tr22** (both, vitals). An adult at rest has a pulse over 100. What is that called?
- Choices: Normal for most adults / **It is called rapid** / Slow, and a bit low
- Fix: A pulse over 100 beats a minute is rapid.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Any pulse rate above 100 beats per minute is rapid"

**tr23** (ya, vitals). Your friend runs track and has a resting pulse of 45. Is that a sign of illness?
- Choices: Yes; call 911 right away / **That can be normal for athletes** / It means she needs a doctor
- Fix: A fit athlete may have a normal resting pulse of 40 to 50.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "An athlete may have a normal at-rest pulse rate between 40 and 50 beats per minute."

**tr24** (both, vitals). How many breaths a minute is normal for an adult at rest?
- Choices: About 30 to 40 / **About 12 to 20** / About 4 to 8 or so
- Fix: A resting adult takes 12 to 20 breaths a minute.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "The normal respiration rate for an adult at rest is 12 to 20 breaths per minute."

**tr25** (both, vitals). What is a quick way to count breaths?
- Choices: Ask how they feel breathing / **Count 30 seconds, times 2** / Count 10 seconds, times 3
- Fix: Count breaths for 30 seconds and multiply by 2.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Count the number of breaths taken by the patient during 30 seconds and multiply by 2"

**tr26** (both, vitals). A hurt person breathes very fast, or very slow. What does that mean?
- Choices: They are only scared; wait / **They need fast medical care** / They will settle down soon
- Fix: Breathing much too fast or too slow means they need medical care fast.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "A rapid or slow respiration rate indicates the patient is in need of immediate medical attention"

**tr27** (both, vitals). You have no thermometer. How do you get a quick sense of skin temperature?
- Choices: Feel the palm of their hand / **Back of your hand on forehead** / Feel the bottom of a foot
- Fix: Feel the forehead with the back of your hand. Note warm, hot, cool or cold.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "forehead with the back of your hand"

**tr28** (both, shock). After a car wreck, a man gets restless and anxious. What could it be?
- Choices: Only fear; he will be fine / **An early sign of shock** / Low sugar; give him soda
- Fix: Restlessness and worry are early signs of shock. Call 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Restlessness and apprehension are early symptoms, often followed by apathy."

**tr29** (both, shock). What does skin often feel like in shock?
- Choices: Hot, dry and flushed / **Cool, with clammy sweat** / Normal, warm and pink
- Fix: In shock, skin feels cool and is covered with clammy sweat.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "The skin feels cool and is covered with clammy sweat."

**tr30** (both, shock). What is the pulse often like in shock?
- Choices: Slow and very strong / **Rapid, weak, and thready** / Steady and quite normal
- Fix: The pulse often gets rapid, weak and thready.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "The pulse tends to become rapid, weak, and thready."

**tr31** (both, shock). One sign of shock looks fine, but the rest of the scene looks bad. What wins?
- Choices: The one sign that looks fine / **The whole picture of the scene** / The person's own word on it
- Fix: The whole picture matters more than any one sign.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Evaluation of the whole situation is more important than one particular sign or symptom."

**tr32** (both, shock). A blood pressure cuff shows the top number under 90. What is that?
- Choices: Normal for a calm person / **Low blood pressure** / High blood pressure
- Fix: A top number below 90 is low blood pressure, which can be a sign of shock.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "when the systolic pressure falls below 90"

**tr35** (ya, rescue). A friend is drowning at the lake. You are not trained in lifesaving. Best way to help?
- Choices: Swim out and grab them / **Hold out a pole or branch** / Wait on shore, call later
- Fix: Hold out a pole, oar, branch or stick, or throw something that floats. Call 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "holding out a pole, oar, branch, or stick for the victim to catch hold of"

**tr36** (ya, rescue). Why is it risky to swim out to a drowning person if you are not trained?
- Choices: The water may be cold / **They may panic and fight you** / You may lose your shoes
- Fix: A drowning person may panic and fight so hard that you both go under.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "A drowning person may panic and fight against you"

**tr37** (both, rescue). There is smoke and you have no mask. Does a wet cloth keep you safe from bad gases?
- Choices: Yes; it blocks all gas / **It only cuts smoke** / Only if kept very wet
- Fix: A wet cloth helps with smoke and heat, but gives no protection from poison gas or low oxygen.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "A WET CLOTH GIVES YOU NO PROTECTION AGAINST POISONOUS GASES"

**tr38** (both, rescue). In a smoky room, is the air near the floor safe from deadly gases?
- Choices: Clean air sits down low / **Deadly gas can be low too** / Gas always rises to the top
- Fix: There is less smoke low, but deadly gases like carbon monoxide can be down low too.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "are just as likely to be present near the floor as near the"

**tr39** (both, rescue). There is a fire in the house. Before you open a closed door, what do you do?
- Choices: Open it fast to look / **Feel the door first** / Kick it open hard
- Fix: Always feel a door first. If it is hot, use another way out.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Always feel a door before you open it."

**tr40** (both, rescue). Your clothes catch fire and you roll up in a blanket. What about your head?
- Choices: Wrap it up tight too / **Your head stays uncovered** / Pour water on it first
- Fix: Roll up in a blanket, coat or rug, but keep your head uncovered.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "KEEP YOUR HEAD UNCOVERED."

**tr41** (ya, rescue). You are on an upper floor of a burning building. When is jumping okay?
- Choices: At once, if the floor is hot / **Only as a last resort** / Any time it seems faster
- Fix: Do not jump from an upper floor except as a last resort.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not jump from an upper floor of a burning building except as a last resort."

**tr42** (adult, rescue). A neighbor collapses inside a grain bin or tank. Will a wet rag over your face keep you safe going in?
- Choices: Yes; a wet rag works fine / **It won't; air may lack oxygen** / Only if you hold your breath
- Fix: A mask or wet cloth will not protect you from low oxygen or poison gas. Call 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Do not depend upon a protective mask or a wet cloth held over your face to protect you from oxygen deficiency or poisonous gases."

**tr43** (adult, rescue). You smell gas in a shed. You need light to look around. What do you bring?
- Choices: A lighter, just for a moment / **Nothing; get out and call** / A candle in a glass jar
- Fix: Never bring open flames, lighters or ordinary lights near possible explosive gas. Get out and call.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Matches, cigarette lighters, flashlights, candles or other open flames, and ordinary electrical lights must NEVER be taken into a compartment that may contain explosive vapors."

**tr44** (both, moving). You carry a hurt person on a stretcher. Which way do they go?
- Choices: Head first, so they see / **Feet first, always** / Either way is fine
- Fix: Move them feet first so the back carrier can watch their breathing.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Always move the victim FEET FIRST so the rear bearer can watch for signs of breathing difficulty."

**tr45** (both, moving). You have a stretcher and a hurt person. Who moves to whom?
- Choices: Carry the person to it / **Bring it to them** / Have them walk to it
- Fix: When you can, bring the stretcher to the hurt person.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "When possible, bring the stretcher to the casualty."

**tr46** (both, moving). A hurt person is safe where they lie. Should you move them before you check them?
- Choices: Move them to a soft bed / **Check them first** / Drive them in right now
- Fix: Unless the spot is unsafe, check and treat before you move them.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Casualties should not be moved before the type and extent of injuries are evaluated"

**tr47** (both, moving). A badly hurt person is in the water. Should you haul them out with a rope?
- Choices: Yes; it's the fastest way / **Only in a true emergency** / Sure, if two people pull
- Fix: Only in an extreme emergency. Keep them flat and still if you can.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "A seriously injured person should never, except in an extreme emergency, be hauled out of the water by means of a rope or lifeline."

**tr48** (adult, moving). A person is trapped in a wrecked car. What is the first stage of getting them out?
- Choices: Pull them out right away / **Get to them; reach them** / Wait for a tow truck
- Fix: The first stage is gaining access, reaching the person without hurting them more.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "The first stage of extrication within each of the rescue phases outlined above is gaining access to the victim."

**tr49** (both, medical). A man acts drunk: confused and unsteady. What else could it be?
- Choices: Only alcohol; let him sleep / **Diabetes; look for an ID tag** / Only tired; let him rest
- Fix: Diabetic people are often mistaken for drunk. Look for a medical ID and call 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Diabetic victims are often mistakenly treated as if intoxicated"

**tr50** (both, medical). A diabetic friend is awake but acting odd. You don't know if sugar is high or low. Give sugar?
- Choices: No, it could make it worse / **Yes; when in doubt, give sugar** / Wait for it to pass
- Fix: If in doubt, give sugar to a person who is awake, and call 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "If you are in doubt as to whether the victim is in insulin shock or a ketoacidotic state, give them sugar."

**tr51** (adult, medical). A confused person has breath that smells sweet, like nail polish remover. What may it be?
- Choices: Too much candy today / **A diabetic emergency** / Bad breath, nothing more
- Fix: A sweet acetone smell can be a sign of high blood sugar. Call 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "a sickly sweet acetone odor is present on the breath"

**tr52** (both, medical). A person is passed out and no one knows them. What is worth checking for?
- Choices: Their phone's last calls / **A medical ID tag or bracelet** / Their pockets for snacks
- Fix: Look for a medical ID necklace or bracelet. It can tell you about diabetes and more.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Diabetics almost always wear a medical identification device."

**tr53** (adult, medical). Your father's face droops on one side and that arm is weak. It came on all at once. What now?
- Choices: Let him nap it off for now / **Dial 911; may be a stroke** / Give him a big meal first
- Fix: Sudden weakness on one side can be a stroke. Call 911 right away.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Onset of a cerebrovascular accident is sudden, with little or no warning."

**tr54** (adult, medical). While you wait for help with a possible stroke, how do you lay them?
- Choices: Flat on the back, head down / **Half sitting, or on weak side** / Face down on the floor
- Fix: Lay them half sitting, or on the paralyzed side, and watch the airway.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Place the victim in a semi-reclining position or on the paralyzed side."

**tr55** (both, medical). A man has had a drug many times with no trouble. Can it still cause a severe allergic reaction?
- Choices: No, he is safe by now / **Yes, it can happen** / Only the first time
- Fix: A severe allergic reaction can happen at any time, even after many safe uses.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "Anaphylaxis can happen at any time, even to people who have taken penicillin many times before without experiencing any problems."

**tr56** (adult, medical). A child swallowed something from under the sink. What matters most at first?
- Choices: Name the exact poison first / **Care for breathing; call help** / Make the child throw up
- Fix: Caring for airway, breathing and circulation matters most. Call Poison Control or 911.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "is safe and effective in the vast majority of poisonings."

**tr57** (adult, medical). Your teen swallowed cleaner but feels fine. Can you skip calling for help?
- Choices: Yes, no signs means fine / **Get help; signs can worsen fast** / Give milk and then just wait
- Fix: Mild signs can get worse fast. Call Poison Control and watch closely.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "A patient with mild symptoms may deteriorate rapidly, so observe them closely."

**tr58** (both, medical). What is the US Poison Control phone number?
- Choices: Dial 411 and ask / **1-800-222-1222** / Dial 311 and ask
- Fix: In the US, call Poison Control at 1-800-222-1222. Call 911 if they are not breathing or passed out.
- Source: `chemm-start` (https://chemm.hhs.gov/incident-primer/triage/start-triage) quote: "call Poison Control at 1-800-222-1222"

**tr59** (ya, lightning). You are caught in a field as a storm comes. Does crouching down make you safe?
- Choices: Yes; crouching keeps you low / **Run for a building or car** / Crouch on tiptoes to be safe
- Fix: Crouching does not make you safer. Get to a solid building or hard-top car.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "Crouching doesn't make you any safer outdoors."

**tr60** (ya, lightning). The storm is still miles away and the sky above you is blue. Are you safe from lightning?
- Choices: Safe; there is no rain here / **It can strike 10 miles off** / Safe, if no clouds are above you
- Fix: Lightning can strike 10 to 15 miles from the storm.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "can strike 10-15 miles from the thunderstorm"

**tr61** (ya, lightning). Rain starts with thunder on a hike. Is a big tree a good place to wait?
- Choices: Good; it keeps you dry / **Bad; trees draw many strikes** / Fine, if it is the tallest
- Fix: Being under a tree is the second leading cause of lightning injury.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "Being underneath a tree is the second leading cause of lightning casualties."

**tr62** (ya, lightning). Lightning is close and you are stuck outside. Should you lie flat on the ground?
- Choices: Yes; it keeps you low / **Keep moving toward shelter** / Lie face down; that is best
- Fix: Lying flat raises your risk from ground current. Keep moving toward shelter.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "Lying flat increases your chance of being affected by potentially deadly ground current."

**tr63** (ya, lightning). Your team is one point from winning when thunder rolls. Finish the game?
- Choices: Finish, then go in / **Go inside right away** / Play on, unless you see a bolt
- Fix: Seek shelter right away when you hear thunder.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "Seek proper shelter immediately if you hear thunder."

**tr64** (ya, lightning). Does a phone or a metal watch draw lightning to you?
- Choices: Metal pulls it in / **Metal makes no difference at all** / Phones draw it most of all
- Fix: Height, shape and being alone in the open matter. Metal makes no difference.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "The presence of metal makes absolutely no difference on where lightning strikes."

**tr65** (adult, lightning). You are inside during a thunderstorm. What should you stay off?
- Choices: Your cell phone and the couch / **Corded phones and plumbing** / Rugs and wooden chairs
- Fix: Stay off corded phones, appliances, plumbing and metal doors and windows.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "staying off corded phones"

**tr66** (ya, lightning). A tall flagpole was struck last year. Will lightning skip it now?
- Choices: It won't strike twice, ever / **It often hits again** / It skips it for 10 years
- Fix: Lightning often strikes the same place again, mostly tall, pointy, alone objects.
- Source: `noaa-lightning` (https://www.weather.gov/safety/lightning-myths) quote: "Lightning often strikes the same place repeatedly"

**tr67** (adult, assess). A person feels sick, with no injury. Which part of your check matters most?
- Choices: The hands-on exam, mostly / **Talking with them, mostly** / Taking a photo to send later
- Fix: For illness, the check is mostly talk (about 80 percent) and less exam.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "the medical assessment is 80 percent patient interview and 20 percent physical exam."

**tr68** (adult, assess). Why ask a sick person when they last ate or drank?
- Choices: To plan a snack for them / **Food can cause or worsen it** / It is only some small talk
- Fix: Food could cause the problem, and the hospital needs to know before surgery.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "When did you eat or drink last?"

**tr69** (adult, assess). You kneel to talk to a hurt person in bright sun. Where should the sun be?
- Choices: Behind you, to shade them well / **Off to the side, not behind you** / Above them, in their eyes
- Fix: Do not let the sun or bright lights be at your back, so they can see your face.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "position yourself so that the sun or bright lights are not at your back."

**tr70** (adult, assess). You help a hurt teen who is not yours. What should you always ask?
- Choices: What school do you attend / **How to reach a parent** / What is your full name
- Fix: With a minor, always ask how to reach their parent or guardian.
- Source: `navedtra-14295` (https://navytribe.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/11/navedtra-14295.pdf) quote: "With minors, always ask how you can contact their parent or guardian."

**tr71** (both, triage). In START triage, which breathing rate is a key cutoff?
- Choices: 10 breaths a minute / **30 breaths a minute** / 60 breaths a minute
- Fix: START checks if breathing is more or less than 30 times a minute. Red means problems with airway, breathing or blood flow.
- Source: `remm-start` (https://remm.hhs.gov/startalgotext.htm) quote: "Respiratory rate greater or less than 30 per minute" and "Immediate: Red Triage Tag Color ... Includes compromise to patient's airway, breathing, and circulation"
